Since 2016, France is divided into 18 administrative regions: 13 regions in metropolitan France (including Corsica),[^cite_note-144] and five overseas.[^cite_note-France-100] The regions are further subdivided into 101 [departments](38523.md),[^cite_note-145] which are numbered mainly alphabetically. The department number is used in postal codes and was formerly used on [vehicle registration plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_registration_plates_of_France). Among the 101 French departments, five ([French Guiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Guiana), [Guadeloupe](12343.md), [Martinique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinique), [Mayotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayotte), and [Réunion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A9union)) are in overseas regions that are simultaneously overseas departments, enjoying the same status as metropolitan departments and are thereby included in the European Union.&#x20;

The 101 departments are subdivided into 335 [arrondissements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrondissements_of_France), which are, in turn, subdivided into 2,054 [cantons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantons_of_France).[^cite_note-constituencies-146] These cantons are divided into 36,658 [communes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communes_of_France), which are municipalities with an elected municipal council.[^cite_note-constituencies-146] Three communes—Paris, Lyon and Marseille—are subdivided into 45 [municipal arrondissements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_arrondissements_of_France).&#x20;

#### Overseas territories and collectivities

In addition to the 18 regions and 101 departments, the French Republic has five overseas collectivities ([French Polynesia](10737.md), [Saint Barthélemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy), [Saint Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin), [Saint Pierre and Miquelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon), and [Wallis and Futuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallis_and_Futuna)), one *[sui generis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sui_generis)* collectivity ([New Caledonia](21342.md)), one [overseas territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_territory_\(France\)) ([French Southern and Antarctic Lands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Southern_and_Antarctic_Lands)), and one island possession in the Pacific Ocean (Clipperton Island). Overseas collectivities and territories form part of the French Republic but do not form part of the European Union or its fiscal area (except for Saint Barthélemy, which seceded from Guadeloupe in 2007). The Pacific Collectivities of French Polynesia, Wallis and Futuna, and New Caledonia continue to use the [CFP franc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CFP_franc)[^cite_note-147] whose value is strictly linked to the euro. The five overseas regions use the euro.[^cite_note-148]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_France)

France is a founding member of the United Nations and serves as one of the [permanent members of the UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_members_of_the_United_Nations_Security_Council) with veto rights.[^cite_note-149] In 2015 it was described as "the best networked state in the world" due to its membership in more international institutions than any other country;[^cite_note-150] these include the G7, [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization),[^cite_note-151] the [Pacific Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Community)[^cite_note-152] and the [Indian Ocean Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Ocean_Commission).[^cite_note-153] It is an associate member of the [Association of Caribbean States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Caribbean_States)[^cite_note-154] and a leading member of the [Organisation internationale de la Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie) of 84 French-speaking countries.[^cite_note-155]&#x20;

La Francophonie map (dozens of countries in Africa, Europe, Asia and Latin America are members of this international organisation.

88 states and governments are part of *[La Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Francophonie)*,

[^cite_note-156]

&#x20;which promotes values of [democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy), [multilingualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multilingualism) and [cultural diversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_diversity).

[^cite_note-157]

&#x20;France has been a key member of this global organisation since its inception in 1970.

European Parliament opening in Strasbourg with crowd and many countries' flags on flagpoles

The [European Parliament](9581.md) in [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg) (near the border with Germany). France is a founding member of all EU institutions.

As a significant hub for international relations, France has the [third-largest assembly](6731624.md) of [diplomatic missions](8970.md), behind China and the United States. It also hosts the headquarters of several [international organisations](18201224.md), including the [OECD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development), [UNESCO](21786641.md), [Interpol](14338129.md), the [International Bureau of Weights and Measures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Bureau_of_Weights_and_Measures), and the OIF.[^cite_note-158]&#x20;

French foreign policy after World War II has been largely shaped by membership in the European Union, of which it was a [founding member](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_Six). Since the 1963 [Élysée Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lys%C3%A9e_Treaty), France [has developed close ties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%E2%80%93Germany_relations) with reunified Germany to become the most influential driving force of the EU.[^cite_note-159] Since 1904 France has maintained an "[Entente Cordiale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entente_Cordiale)" with the United Kingdom, and there has been a strengthening of [ties between the countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_relations), [especially militarily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster_House_Treaties).&#x20;

France is a member of the North Atlantic Treaty Organization ([NATO](21133.md)), but under President [Charles de Gaulle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle) excluded itself from the joint military command in protest of the [Special Relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Relationship) between the U.S. and UK, and to preserve the independence of French foreign and security policies. Under [Nicolas Sarkozy](557007.md), France rejoined the NATO joint military command.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162]&#x20;

France retains strong political and economic influence in its former African colonies (*[Françafrique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7afrique)*)[^cite_note-163] and has supplied economic aid and troops for peacekeeping missions in [Ivory Coast](57744.md) and [Chad](5488.md).[^cite_note-164] From 2012 to 2021, France and other African states intervened in support of the Malian government in the [Mali War](36856978.md).&#x20;

In 2017, France was the world's fourth-largest donor of [development aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Development_aid) in absolute terms.[^cite_note-165] This represents 0.43% of its [GNP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNP).[^cite_note-166] Aid is provided by the governmental [French Development Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Development_Agency), which finances primarily humanitarian projects in [sub-Saharan Africa](27067.md),[^cite_note-aid-167] with an emphasis on "developing infrastructure, access to health care and education, the implementation of appropriate economic policies and the consolidation of the rule of law and democracy".[^cite_note-aid-167]&#x20;

### Military

The French Armed Forces (*Forces armées françaises*) are the military and paramilitary forces of France, under the president as commander-in-chief. They consist of the [French Army](611740.md) (*Armée de Terre*), the [French Navy](383256.md) (*Marine Nationale*, formerly called *Armée de Mer*), the [French Air and Space Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Air_and_Space_Force) (*Armée de l'Air et de l'Espace*), and the [National Gendarmerie](6330819.md) (*Gendarmerie nationale*), which serves as both [military police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_police) and civil police in rural areas. Together they are among the [largest armed forces in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_military_and_paramilitary_personnel) and the largest in the EU. According to a 2015 study by [Crédit Suisse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cr%C3%A9dit_Suisse), the French Armed Forces ranked as the world's sixth-most powerful military and the second most powerful in Europe.[^cite_note-CreditSuisse2015-168] France's annual military expenditure in 2026 is US\$65.3 billion, or 2.1% [of its GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_with_highest_military_expenditures), making it the eighth biggest military spender in the world.[^cite_note-169] There has been no national conscription since 1997.[^cite_note-170]&#x20;



&#x20; France

&#x20; French military interventions since 2001: [Afghanistan](19666611.md); [Ivory Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_French%E2%80%93Ivorian_clashes); [Chad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chadian_Civil_War_\(2005%E2%80%9310\)); [Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya); [Somalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Enduring_Freedom_%E2%80%93_Horn_of_Africa); [Mali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Mali_conflict); [Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Republic_conflict_under_the_Djotodia_administration); [Syria](30741795.md); [Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American-led_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%93present\)).

France has been a [recognised nuclear state](400821.md#Five_nuclear-weapon_states_under_the_NPT) since 1960. It is a party to both the [Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Nuclear-Test-Ban_Treaty)[^cite_note-status-171] and the [Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty](22107.md). The French nuclear force (formerly known as "*[Force de Frappe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Force_de_dissuasion)*") consists of four [*Triomphant*-class submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triomphant-class_submarine) equipped with [submarine-launched ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine-launched_ballistic_missile). France has about 60 [ASMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air-sol_moyenne_port%C3%A9e) medium-range [air-to-ground missiles](1544116.md) with [nuclear warheads](21785.md);[^cite_note-172] 50 are deployed by the Air and Space Force using the [Mirage 2000N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Mirage_2000N/2000D) long-range nuclear strike aircraft, while around 10 are deployed by the French Navy's [*Super Étendard Modernisé*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault-Breguet_Super_%C3%89tendard) attack aircraft, which operate from the nuclear-powered aircraft carrier [*Charles de Gaulle*](639127.md).&#x20;

France has major military industries and one of the largest [aerospace sectors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace_manufacturer) in the world.[^cite_note-173] The country has produced such equipment as the [Dassault Rafale](33731380.md) fighter, the *Charles de Gaulle* aircraft carrier, the [Exocet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exocet) missile, and the [Leclerc tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leclerc_tank), among others. France is a major arms seller,[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] with most of its arsenal's designs available for the export market, except for nuclear-powered devices. Weapons exported totalled 27 billion euros in 2022, up from 11.7 billion euros the previous year. The [UAE](69328.md) contributed more than 16 billion euros arms to the French total.[^cite_note-176] Among the largest French defence companies are [Dassault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Group), [Thales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thales_Group) and [Safran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safran).[^cite_note-177]&#x20;

The [Directorate General for External Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directorate_General_for_External_Security) is a component of the Armed Forces under the authority of the [Ministry of Defence](37707412.md). The [General Directorate for Internal Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Directorate_for_Internal_Security) operates under the authority of the [Ministry of the Interior](871703.md).[^cite_note-dgsi-178] France's cybersecurity capabilities are regularly ranked as some of the most robust of any nation in the world.[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180]&#x20;

### Law

Main article: [Law of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_France)

See also: [Administrative jurisdiction in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_jurisdiction_in_France)

France uses a [civil legal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) system, wherein law arises primarily from written statutes;[^cite_note-France-100] judges are not to make law, but merely to interpret it (though the amount of judicial interpretation in certain areas makes it equivalent to [case law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case_law) in a [common law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_law) system). Basic principles of the [rule of law](25166191.md) were laid in the [Napoleonic Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_Code) (which was largely based on royal law codified under King Louis XIV). In agreement with the principles of the Declaration of the Rights of Man and of the Citizen, the law should only prohibit actions detrimental to society.&#x20;

color drawing of the Declaration of the Rights of Man and of the Citizen from 1789

The basic principles that the French Republic must respect are found in the 1789 [Declaration of the Rights of Man and of the Citizen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_the_Rights_of_Man_and_of_the_Citizen).

French law is divided into two principal areas: [private law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_law) and [public law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_law). Private law includes, in particular, [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(common_law\)) and [criminal law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_law). Public law includes, in particular, [administrative law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_law) and [constitutional law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_law). However, in practical terms, French law comprises three principal areas of law: civil law, criminal law, and administrative law. Criminal laws can only address the future and not the past (criminal *[ex post facto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ex_post_facto_law)* laws are prohibited).[^cite_note-181] While administrative law is often a subcategory of civil law in many countries, it is completely separated in France and each body of law is headed by a specific supreme court: [ordinary courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_judiciary_courts) (which handle criminal and civil litigation) are headed by the [Court of Cassation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Cassation_\(France\)) and administrative courts are headed by the [Council of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_d%27Etat_\(France\)). To be applicable, every law must be officially published in the *[Journal officiel de la République française](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journal_officiel_de_la_R%C3%A9publique_fran%C3%A7aise)*.&#x20;

### Human rights

Main article: [Human rights in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_France)

France does not recognise [religious law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_law) as a motivation for the enactment of prohibitions; it has long abolished [blasphemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blasphemy) laws and [sodomy laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sodomy_law). However, "offences against public [decency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decency)" or [disturbing public order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breach_of_the_peace) have been used to repress public expressions of homosexuality or street prostitution.[^cite_note-182]&#x20;

France generally has a positive reputation regarding [LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_France).[^cite_note-183] Since 1999, [civil unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacte_civil_de_solidarit%C3%A9) for homosexual couples have been permitted, and since 2013, [same-sex marriage](92656.md) and [LGBT adoption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_adoption) are legal.[^cite_note-184] France has laws against racism and [antisemitism](1078.md),[^cite_note-185] while the 1990 [Gayssot Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gayssot_Act) prohibits [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial). In 2024, France became the first nation in the European Union to explicitly protect [abortion](765.md) in its [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_France).[^cite_note-186]&#x20;

[Freedom of religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_France) is constitutionally guaranteed by the 1789 Declaration of the Rights of Man and of the Citizen. The [1905 French law on the Separation of the Churches and the State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1905_French_law_on_the_Separation_of_the_Churches_and_the_State) is the basis for *[laïcité](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La%C3%AFcit%C3%A9)* (state secularism): the state does not formally recognise any religion, [except in Alsace-Moselle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concordat_in_Alsace-Moselle), which continues to subsidise education and clergy of Catholicism, Lutheranism, Calvinism, and Judaism. Nonetheless, France does recognise religious associations. The Parliament has [listed many religious movements as dangerous cults](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groups_referred_to_as_cults_in_government_reports#France) since 1995 and has [banned wearing conspicuous religious symbols in schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_law_on_secularity_and_conspicuous_religious_symbols_in_schools) since 2004. In 2010, it banned the [wearing of face-covering Islamic veils in public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_ban_on_face_covering); human rights groups such as [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) described the law as discriminatory towards Muslims.[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188] However, it is supported by most of the population.[^cite_note-189]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_France)

Further information: [Science and technology in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_France)

La Défense, seen from the Eiffel Tower

[La Défense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_D%C3%A9fense) was in 2017 ranked by [Ernst & Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_%26_Young) as the leading [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_business_district) in continental Europe, and fourth in the world.

[^cite_note-190]

France has a [social market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_market_economy) characterised by sizeable government involvement and [diversified sectors](63252200.md). For two centuries, it has [consistently ranked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_largest_historical_GDP) among the ten largest globally; as of 2025 it is the [world's ninth largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)) by [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity) and second largest in the EU, after Germany.[^cite_note-data.worldbank.org-191] Considered a [great power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_power) with considerable [economic strength](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_power),[^cite_note-192] it is a member of the [Group of Seven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_Seven) leading industrialised countries, the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](33853117.md) (OECD), and the [G20](2206608.md). France ranked 13th in the 2025 [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index).[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194]&#x20;

The economy is highly diversified; services represent two-thirds of both the workforce and GDP,[^cite_note-195] while the industrial sector accounts for a fifth of GDP and a similar proportion of employment. France is the third-biggest manufacturing country in Europe, behind Germany and Italy, and ranks [eighth in the world by manufacturing output](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_manufacturing_output), at 1.9 per cent.[^cite_note-196] Less than 2 per cent of GDP is generated by the primary sector, namely agriculture;[^cite_note-197] the agricultural sector is among the largest in value and leads the EU in overall production.[^cite_note-198]&#x20;

In 2018 France was the [fifth-largest trading nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_total_trade) and second-largest in Europe, with the value of exports representing over a fifth of GDP.[^cite_note-wto-199] Its membership in the [eurozone](184391.md) and the broader [European single market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_single_market) facilitates access to capital, goods, services, and skilled labour.[^cite_note-euroc-200] Despite [protectionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism) policies over certain industries, particularly in agriculture, France has generally played a leading role in fostering free trade and commercial integration in Europe to enhance its economy.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] In 2019, it ranked first in Europe and [13th in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_foreign_direct_investment_inflows) in [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment), with European countries and the United States being leading sources.[^cite_note-FDI-203] According to the [Bank of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_France) (founded in 1800),[^cite_note-204] the leading recipients of foreign direct investment are manufacturing, real estate, finance and insurance.[^cite_note-lloydsbanktrade.com-205] The [Paris Region](1716427.md) has the highest concentration of multinational firms in mainland Europe.[^cite_note-lloydsbanktrade.com-205]&#x20;

Under the doctrine of *[dirigisme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirigisme)*, the government historically played a major role in the economy; policies such as [indicative planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indicative_planning) and [nationalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalization) are credited for contributing to three decades of unprecedented postwar economic growth known as *[Trente Glorieuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trente_Glorieuses)*. At its peak in 1982, the public sector accounted for one-fifth of industrial employment and over four-fifths of the credit market. Beginning in the late 20th century, regulations and state involvement in the economy were loosened, with most leading companies becoming privately owned; state ownership still dominates transportation, defence and broadcasting.[^cite_note-206] Policies aimed at promoting economic dynamism and privatisation have improved France's economic standing globally: it is among the world's 10 most innovative countries in the 2020 [Bloomberg Innovation Index](432006.md),[^cite_note-207] and the 15th most competitive according to the 2019 [Global Competitiveness Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Competitiveness_Report#2019_rankings).[^cite_note-208]&#x20;

The [Paris stock exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronext_Paris) is one of the oldest in the world, created in 1724.[^cite_note-209] Some examples of the most valuable French companies include [LVMH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LVMH), [L'Oréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%27Or%C3%A9al) and [Sociéte Générale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9rale).[^cite_note-210] France has historically been one of the world's major agricultural centres and remains a "global agricultural powerhouse"; France is the world's sixth-biggest exporter of agricultural products, generating a trade surplus of over €7.4 billion.[^cite_note-agriculture-211][^cite_note-212] Nicknamed "the granary of the old continent",[^cite_note-213] over half its total land area is farmland, of which 45 per cent is devoted to permanent field crops such as cereals. The country's diverse climate, extensive arable land, modern farming technology, and [EU subsidies](141924.md) have made it Europe's leading agricultural producer and exporter.[^cite_note-214]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_France)



[Palace of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles), [Yvelines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yvelines)

With 102 million international tourist arrivals in 2025, France is the [world's top tourist destination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Tourism_rankings)[^cite_note-215] and ranks third in tourism-derived income due to shorter visit durations.[^cite_note-216] The most popular tourist sites include (annual visitors): [Eiffel Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eiffel_Tower) (6.2 million), [Château de Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles) (2.8 million), [Muséum national d'Histoire naturelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Natural_History_\(France\)) (2 million), [Pont du Gard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pont_du_Gard) (1.5 million), [Arc de Triomphe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arc_de_Triomphe) (1.2 million), [Mont Saint-Michel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mont_Saint-Michel) (1 million), [Sainte-Chapelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sainte-Chapelle) (683,000), [Château du Haut-Kœnigsbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%A2teau_du_Haut-K%C5%93nigsbourg) (549,000), [Puy de Dôme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puy_de_D%C3%B4me) (500,000), [Musée Picasso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_Picasso) (441,000), and [Carcassonne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carcassonne) (362,000).[^cite_note-217]&#x20;

France, especially Paris, has some of the world's largest museums including the [Louvre](17546.md), which is the [most visited art museum in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most-visited_art_museums) (7.7 million visitors in 2022); the [Musée d'Orsay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_d%27Orsay) (3.3 million), mostly devoted to [Impressionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionism) and voted best museum in the world in 2018;[^cite_note-218] the [Musée de l'Orangerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mus%C3%A9e_de_l%27Orangerie) (1.02 million), which is home to eight large [Water Lily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_Lilies_\(Monet_series\)) murals by [Claude Monet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claude_Monet); and the [Centre Pompidou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_Pompidou) (3 million), dedicated to [contemporary art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_art).&#x20;

[Disneyland Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disneyland_Paris) is Europe's most popular theme park, with 15 million combined visitors to the resort's [Disneyland Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disneyland_Park_\(Paris\)) and [Walt Disney Studios Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Studios_Park) in 2009.[^cite_note-219] With more than 10 million tourists per year, the [French Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Riviera) is the second leading tourist destination in the country, after the Paris Region.[^cite_note-220] With 6 million tourists per year, the [castles of the Loire Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%A2teaux_of_the_Loire_Valley) (French: *châteaux*) and the [Loire Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loire_Valley) are the third leading tourist destination.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222]&#x20;

France has [54 sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_France) inscribed on the [UNESCO World Heritage List](44940.md#Statistics), ranking it fourth worldwide by number of sites.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224] France features cities of high cultural interest, beaches and seaside resorts, ski resorts, as well as rural regions that many enjoy for their beauty and tranquillity ([green tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecotourism)). Small and picturesque villages are promoted through the association *[Les Plus Beaux Villages de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Plus_Beaux_Villages_de_France)* (literally "The Most Beautiful Villages of France"). The "[Remarkable Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Remarkable_Gardens_of_France)" label is a list of the over 200 gardens classified by the [Ministry of Culture](3354559.md). This label is intended to protect and promote remarkable gardens and parks. France attracts many religious [pilgrims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_pilgrimage) on their [way to St. James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camino_de_Santiago) or to [Lourdes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lourdes), a town in the [Hautes-Pyrénées](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hautes-Pyr%C3%A9n%C3%A9es) that hosts several million visitors a year.&#x20;

### Energy

Further information: [Energy in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_France)

Nuclear power plant in Cattenom, France four large cooling towers expelling white water vapour against a blue sky

[Belleville Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belleville_Nuclear_Power_Plant). France derives most of its electricity from [nuclear power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_France), the [highest percentage in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_by_country).

France is the world's tenth-largest producer of electricity.[^cite_note-225] [Électricité de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lectricit%C3%A9_de_France) (EDF), which is majority-owned by the French government, is the country's main producer and distributor of electricity and one of the world's largest [electric utility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_utility) companies, ranking third in revenue globally.[^cite_note-226] In 2018, EDF produced roughly one-fifth of the EU's electricity, primarily from [nuclear power](22153.md).[^cite_note-227] In 2021, France was the biggest energy exporter in Europe, mostly to the UK and Italy,[^cite_note-world-nuclear.org-228] and the largest net exporter of electricity in the world.[^cite_note-world-nuclear.org-228]&#x20;

Since the [1973 oil crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_oil_crisis), France has pursued a strong policy of [energy security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_security),[^cite_note-world-nuclear.org-228] namely through [heavy investment in nuclear energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_France). It is one of 32 countries with [nuclear power plants](207347.md), ranking second in the world by the [number of operational nuclear reactors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_by_country) at 56.[^cite_note-229] Consequently, 70% of its electricity is generated by nuclear power, the highest proportion in the world by a wide margin;[^cite_note-230] only Slovakia and Ukraine also derive a majority of electricity from nuclear power, at roughly 53% and 51%, respectively.[^cite_note-231] France is considered a world leader in nuclear technology, with reactors and fuel products being major exports.[^cite_note-world-nuclear.org-228]&#x20;

The significant reliance on nuclear power has resulted in comparatively slower [adoption of renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_France) relative to other Western nations. Nevertheless, between 2008 and 2019, its production capacity from renewable energies rose consistently and nearly doubled.[^cite_note-Statista-232] [Hydropower](14073.md) is by far the leading source, accounting for over half of renewable energy sources[^cite_note-hydropower.org-233] and contributing 13% of its electricity[^cite_note-Statista-232] the third highest proportion in Europe.[^cite_note-hydropower.org-233] Most hydroelectric plants, such as [Eguzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eguzon_dam), [Étang de Soulcem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89tang_de_Soulcem), and [Lac de Vouglans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lac_de_Vouglans), are managed by EDF.[^cite_note-hydropower.org-233] France aims to further expand hydropower into 2040.[^cite_note-Statista-232]&#x20;

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_France)



[Millau Viaduct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millau_Viaduct) is the [tallest bridge in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_bridges).

[^cite_note-structurae-234]

The [railway network in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_France) stretches 29,473 kilometres (18,314 mi) as of 2008[^cite_note-235] and is the second most extensive in Western Europe after [Germany's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Germany).[^cite_note-236] It is operated by the [SNCF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SNCF), and high-speed trains include [Thalys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalys), [Eurostar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurostar) and [TGV](57212.md), which travels at 320 km/h (199 mph).[^cite_note-237] Eurostar and [LeShuttle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeShuttle) connect with the United Kingdom through the [Channel Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_Tunnel). Rail connections exist to all other neighbouring countries in Europe except Andorra. Intra-urban connections are also well developed, with most major cities having [underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) or tramway services complementing bus services.&#x20;

There are approximately 1,027,183 kilometres (638,262 mi) of serviceable roadway, ranking it the most extensive network of the European continent.[^cite_note-238] The Paris Region is enveloped with a dense network of roads and highways, which connect it with virtually all parts of the country. Roads also handle substantial international traffic, connecting with cities in neighbouring countries. There is no annual registration fee or [road tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_tax); usage of the mostly privately owned motorways is through tolls except in the vicinity of large communes. The new car market is dominated by domestic brands such as [Renault](162292.md), [Peugeot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peugeot) and [Citroën](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citro%C3%ABn).[^cite_note-239] France possesses the [Millau Viaduct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millau_Viaduct), the world's tallest bridge,[^cite_note-240] and has built many important bridges such as the [Pont de Normandie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pont_de_Normandie). Diesel and petrol-driven cars and lorries cause a large part of the country's air pollution and greenhouse gas emissions.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242]&#x20;

There are 464 [airports in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_France).[^cite_note-France-100] [Charles de Gaulle Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle_Airport), located in the vicinity of Paris, is the largest and busiest airport in the country, handling the vast majority of popular and commercial traffic and connecting Paris with virtually all major cities across the world. [Air France](14998012.md) is the national carrier airline, although numerous private airline companies provide domestic and international travel services. There are ten major ports, the largest of which is in [Marseille](40888948.md),[^cite_note-243] which also is the largest bordering the Mediterranean Sea.[^cite_note-244] 12,261 kilometres (7,619 mi) of waterways traverse France including the [Canal du Midi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canal_du_Midi), which connects the Mediterranean Sea to the Atlantic Ocean through the [Garonne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garonne) river.[^cite_note-France-100]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_France)



Population density in France by [arrondissement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrondissements_of_France)

With an estimated population of 69,081,996 people,[^cite_note-Insee.Population-11][^cite_note-245] France is the [20th most populous country in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population#Sovereign_states_and_dependencies_by_population), the third-most populous in Europe, and the second most populous in the European Union. For much of the 21st century, France has been an outlier among developed countries, particularly in Europe, for its relatively high rate of [natural population growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_of_natural_increase); by birth rates alone, it was responsible for almost all natural population growth in the EU in 2006.[^cite_note-246] Between 2006 and 2016, France saw the second-highest overall increase in population in the EU and was one of four EU countries where natural births accounted for the most population growth.[^cite_note-247] This was the highest rate since the end of the [baby boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_boom) in 1973 and coincides with the rise in the [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) from a nadir of 1.7 in 1994 to 2.0 in 2010.&#x20;

Since 2011, the fertility rate has been steadily declining;[^cite_note-248] it stood at 1.79 per woman in 2023,[^cite_note-249] below the [replacement rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) of 2.1 and well below the high of 4.41 in 1800.[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251][^cite_note-population-252] Fertility rate and crude [birth rate](515898.md) nonetheless remain the highest in the EU[^cite_note-euronews-2023-253] and among the highest in Europe overall, where the average is 1.5.[^cite_note-254] The mean age of French women at the birth of their first child was 29.1, slightly younger than the EU average of 29.7.[^cite_note-euronews-2023-253]&#x20;

Like many developed nations, the [population is aging](720684.md): The average age is 41.7 years, while roughly one-fifth of French people are 65 or over.[^cite_note-255] It is projected that one in three French will be over 60 by 2024. Life expectancy at birth is 82.7 years, the [12th highest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy); French Polynesia and Réunion ranked fourth and 11th in life expectancy, at 84.07 years and 83.55, respectively.&#x20;

From 2006 to 2011, population growth averaged 0.6 percent per year;[^cite_note-evol-256] since 2011, annual growth has been between 0.4 and 0.5 percent annually,[^cite_note-257] and France is projected to continue growing until 2044.[^cite_note-258] Immigrants are major contributors to this trend; in 2010, roughly one in four newborns (27 percent) in Metropolitan France had at least one [foreign-born](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_France#Immigration_per_region) parent, and another 24 percent had at least one parent born outside Europe (excluding French overseas territories).[^cite_note-259] In 2021, the share of children of foreign-born mothers was 23 percent.[^cite_note-euronews-2023-253]&#x20;

The fertility rate in France dropped to 1.56 in 2025. This was the lowest rate since the end of the first world war.[^cite_note-260]&#x20;

### Major cities

See also: [Functional area (France)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_area_\(France\))

France is a highly urbanised country, with its [largest cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_France_over_20,000_population_\(1999_census\)) (in terms of [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_area_\(France\)) population in 2023[^cite_note-261]) being Paris (13,320,752 inh.), [Lyon](8638634.md) (2,339,329), [Marseille](40888948.md) (1,917,728), [Lille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lille) (1,530,624), [Toulouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toulouse) (1,529,112), [Bordeaux](4097.md) (1,426,278), [Nantes](67453.md) (1,050,815), [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg) (875,163), [Montpellier](73369.md) (842,923), and [Rennes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rennes) (789,516). (Note: since its 2020 revision of metropolitan area borders, [INSEE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INSEE) considers that [Nice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nice) is a metropolitan area separate from the [Cannes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannes)-[Antibes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antibes) metropolitan area; these two combined would have a population of 1,045,839, as of the 2023 census). [Rural flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_flight) was a perennial political issue throughout most of the 20th century.&#x20;
